<!-- page 1 -->
NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS’ MINUTES 
TUESDAY, March 12, 2013 
 
PRESENT: Sylvia Allen, Supervisor; Dawnafe Whitesinger, Supervisor; David Tenney, Supervisor; Jesse 
Thompson, Supervisor; and Jonathan M. Nez, Chairman were present in person.  
9:02 a.m.  Call To Order: Pledge of Allegiance and Invocation led by Supervisor Thompson. 
CALL TO THE PUBLIC: There were no public comments. 
 
CONSENT AGENDA: a) Minutes: February 26, 2013 b) Warrant & Voucher Report over $1000 for February 
2013 c) Constable Reports: Snowflake Precinct #3, February  2013 and Show Low Precinct #5, February 2013  
d) Two year reappointment for Connie Bergstrand and Esther Peterson; and a two year appointment for J.R. 
DeSpain to serve on the Navajo County Extension Advisory Board beginning on April 1, 2013 e)  Sympathy 
Letter to President Ben Shelly for the loss of S gt. Jonathon D. Davis, United States Marine Corp f) Special 
Event Liquor License for the Show Low Chamber of Commerce to host a wine tasting and charity even on 
Saturday, April 27, 2013 at the Pinetop Country Club : Supervisor Tenne y made a motion to authorize the 
Chairman to sign the items in the consent agenda as presented ; motion seconded by Supervisor Thompson. 
Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS:  
a. Consideration and possible approval of a Proclamation declaring March 12, 2013  as “Day of the Lady 
Bulldogs”: Supervisor Thompson presented the Proclamation. Supervisor Thompson made a motion 
to approve the Proclamation declaring March 12, 2013 as “Day of the Lady Bulldogs”; motion seconded 
by Supervisor Tenney. Vote unanimous approving the motion. Head Coach Jerron Jordan thanked the 
Board, his team, and assistant coaches : Coach Karen Rockwell, Coach Lynn Sawyer and Coach Eric 
Petranovich. The Coach brought up each student athlete and  each was presented with a medal 
commemorating their winning the State Basketball Tournament Championship.  
b. Consideration and possible approval of a Proclamation declaring March 12, 2013 as “Day of  the Lady 
Bulldogs Dance Team” : Chairman Nez  and Supervisor Thompson congratulated and  presented the 
Proclamation to the team. Supervisor Thomson made a motion to approve the Proclamation declaring 
March 12, 2013 as “Day of the Lady Bulldogs Dance Team” ; motion seconded by Supervisor Tenney. 
Vote unanimous approving the motion. Coach Tamie Ramsey thanked the Board, Coach Carleen Walton 
and her team and presented each girl with a medal commemorating their win.   
EMPLOYEE RECOGNITION:  Navajo County Recognizes Brent Turley  for his excellence in personal 
performance far exceeding organiza tion expectations: LaRee Price recognized Brent Turley for his excellence 
in personal performance. 
HUMAN RESOURCES : Consideration and possible approval of Personnel Actions : Kimberly Eavenson  
reviewed the 13 item personnel action report. Supervisor Thompson made a motion to approve the personnel 
action report as presented; motion seconded by Supervisor Tenney. Vote unanimous approving the motion.

<!-- page 2 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 03-12-2013 
Page 2 of 4 
 
NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC HEALTH SERVICES DISTRICT Board of Directors Session :  Supervisor 
Tenney made a motion  to enter into the Navajo County Public Health Services District Board of Directors 
Session; motion seconded by Supervisor Thompson . Vote unanimous approving the motion. Consent 
Agenda: Approval of ADHS Contract #ADHS13 -041545, the renewal of the Immunizat ion Program, from 
January 1, 2013 to December 31, 2017 : Mary Tyler presented the item.  Supervisor Allen inquired about the 
contract. Mary Tyler responded that these are federal funds contracted with the Arizona Department of Health 
services to provide immu nizations in  Navajo County.  The total grant is dependent of the units  the Health 
Department provides to the children  0 to 18 years of age which are done in immunization clinics in Winslow, 
Holbrook, Snowflake, Show Low and Heber -Overgaard. The Health Depar tment works with the school nurses 
to help administer the immunizations which are required for school entrance. Supervisor Thompson made a 
motion to approve of ADHS Contract #ADHS13 -041545, the renewal of the Immunization Program, from 
January 1, 2013 to D ecember 31, 2017; motion seconded by Supervisor Allen. Vote unanimous approving the 
motion. Supervisor Thompson made a motion  to return to regular session; motion seconded by Supervisor 
Tenney.  Vote unanimous to approving the motion. 
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS/ASSESSOR: Consideration and possible approval of a settlement agreement 
between Snowflake Power v. ADOR & N avajo County in TX -2010-000990: Deputy County Attorney Jason 
Moore discussed the item . He indicated the agreement would reduce the full c ash valuation for Snowflake 
Power from $12,664,000 to $5,500,000  for tax year 2011. The  settlement was dependent upon a  bankruptcy 
trustee’s sale in October 2010. The sale was suggestive of the market value of that property.  Supervisor 
Tenney made a motion  to approve the settlement agreement  between Snowflake Power v. ADOR & Navajo 
County in TX-2010-000990; motion seconded by Supervisor Allen. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
FINANCE:  
a. Consideration and possible approval of an Interlocal Cooperation Contract with the State of Tex as for 
cooperative procurement: Mary Springer presented the item indicating that the contract would offer a 
discounted rate  with different vendors. Supervisor Allen made a motion to approve the  Interlocal 
Cooperation Contract with the State of Texas for coo perative procurement; motion  seconded by 
Supervisor Tenney. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
b. Consideration and possible approval of an extension of professional services agreement with North 
Country Health Care for jail m edical services until May 31, 2013 : Mary Springer presented the item.  
Supervisor Tenney made a motion t o approve the  extension of professional services agreement with 
North Country Health Care for jail medical services  until May 31, 2013; motion seconded by 
Supervisor Allen. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
c. Consideration and possible approval of an AIA Contract Guaranteed Maximum Price (GMP) for 
construction of a new Navajo County Jail services building and retrofit existing areas in the amount of 
$4,895,000: Mary Springer presented the item.  Supervisor Tenney  inquired about the amount as 
suggested compared to the original amount.  Mary responded t hat there have been some additions  since 
the process started, including extensive work on the jail’s security system. Mary told the board the 
needed upgrades added $400,000. Supervisor Allen inquired about the item and what it entailed.   Mary 
indicated this is construction of a new service  building about 10,000 square foot which will have new 
medical, laundry and cooking facilities which will be relocated from the old jail.  It will also have a new 
control center in which the cameras will be updated and the new system would have all security controls 
for the jail in one location. Supervisor Tenney made a motion to approve the AIA Contract Guaranteed 
Maximum Price (GMP) for construction of a new Navajo County Jail services building and retrofit 
existing areas in the a mount of $4,895,000; moti on seconded by  Supervisor Allen . Vote unanimous 
approving the motion.

<!-- page 3 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 03-12-2013 
Page 3 of 4 
 
JUVENILE PROBATION: Consideration and possible approval to authorize Navajo County to be the Fiscal 
Agent for one Federal Grant and one State Grant to sustain operations and substance abuse prevention education 
programs sponsored by the Navajo County Drug Project : Debe Campbell presented the item indicating the drug 
project started in 2003 as a grassroots effort . They are looking for funding in which the county would 
administer as the fis cal agent.  She spoke of the two grants ; the Federal Grant which would help create small 
community coalitions within Navajo County  and the State G rant which would provide  a substance abuse  
program with a parent component.  Supervisor Allen , Supervisor Whitesinger and Supervisor Thompson  
commented on the value, numbers and growth of the program and thanked Debe  Campbell for her efforts . 
Supervisor Tenney made a motion  to approve to authorize Navajo County to be the Fiscal Agent for one 
Federal Grant and one State Grant to sustain operations and substance abuse prevention education programs 
sponsored by the Navajo County Drug Project; motion seconded by Supervisor Whitesinger. Vote unanimous 
approving the motion.  
PUBLIC WORKS:  
a. Consideration and possible approval to hire temporary summer help positions : Homero Vela presented 
the item. Supervisor Tenney  made a motion to approve to hire temporary su mmer help positions; 
motion seconded by Supervisor Allen. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
b. Consideration and possible approval to go out for bids for gravel material for the FEMA approve d 
repairs to Navajo Route 8027 : Homero Vela and Norman Cody presented the item and indicated all 
parties involved have given approval for the project.  Supervisor Thompson made a motion to approve 
to go out for bids for gravel material for the FEMA approved repairs t o Navajo Route 8027; motion 
seconded by Supervisor Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE: Report from Board members, County Attorney and County Manager: James 
Jayne indicated he will attend the County Managers meeting on Friday where they will be going over details of 
the s tate budget . County Attorney Brad Carlyon commented on the NACo Conference in Washington DC ; 
meetings with congressional delegates regarding Secure Rural School funding, PILT and child support funding. 
He reported Supervisor Whitesinger and Sheriff Clark will attend the White Mountain Apache Tribal  Council 
meeting to discuss cross commissioning with the White Mountain Apache Tribe. Chairman Nez commented 
on the Violence Against Women Act that was signed into law. Supervisor Thompson reported on the NACo 
Conference in DC; He reported on the County and Tribal Government Relationships Subcommittee, where they 
discussed working together with government agencies in using IGA’s, MOU’s, and MOA’s to accomplish their 
goals and establish relationships with tribal governments . Supervisor Thompson indicated he also met with 
other congressional delegates, Jeff and Brandi Tomhave in regards to Navajo County issues which included the 
Winslow Levee and the Indian School Bus Route program s. Supervisor Tenney reported on the WIR Board 
meeting, which he was appointed to by the County Supervisors Association, where several reports were given 
on public land issues, presentations with the Department of Agriculture and the Bureau of Land Management on 
how to use GIS better in forest planning. Supervisor Tenney had the opportunity to make visits on Capitol Hill 
with Under Secretary Harris Sherman from the De partment of Agriculture  and U.S. Forest Service Chief Tom 
Tidwell. Discussions of 4FRI, forest planning and travel management plan  were held. Supervisor Tenney and 
Hunter Moore  also met with Senator Flake  and Senator McCain in Washing ton D .C. On the State level 
Supervisor Tenney  reported on his meetings with the CSA staff at  the Senate and the House regarding  the 
budget, restoration of the county  assistance funds, E ECO and HURF funding . Supervisor Whitesinger  
reported on the opportunity to meet  with the White Mountain Apache Tribal Council and is looking at  the 
positive support and direction they will take in the future. She reported on her position on the Sunrise Board and 
that the season has been positive and has seen growth.

<!-- page 4 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 03-12-2013 
Page 4 of 4 
 
Supervisor Whitesinger  indicated she was  recently appointed to the White Mountain Youth Corp ’s Board 
where they are soliciting support and discussing the possibility of providing a  pedestrian path along Porter 
Mountain where the middle school is, in collaboration with the community,  for insuring the safety of the 
children who will utilize that path. She expressed appreciation to those who have given support to her while she 
is continuing to learn . Supervisor Allen reported that she and Deputy County Attorney, Jason Moore  met in 
Cedar Hills with the citizens in that area  to help them with their concerns over  fire districts. She indicated the  
citizens formed a committee. She appreciated  the three fire chiefs from  Snowflake, the White Mountains and 
Taylor being present. Supervisor Allen shared the Auditor General Report of the Navajo County audit for the 
year ended June 30, 2012  to the Board .  She complimented James Menlove and the Navajo County Finance  
team for the success they have received and  the good job that they do  in keeping the books  as reported by the 
State of Arizona Auditor General. Chairman Nez expressed appreciation to the supervisors for their advocacy 
for the constituents of Navajo County, as well as staff and other Navajo County elected officials. 
Navajo Word of the Day: Béésh sinil which means Winslow. 
ADJOURN: At 10:27  a.m. Supervisor Thompson  made a motion  to adjourn; motion seconded by 
Supervisor Tenney. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
 
 
 
 
 
APPROVED: 
 
Jesse Thompson, Vice Chairman 
 
 
ATTEST:  
 
 
 
 
DATE:  March 26, 2013 
 
Melissa W. Buckley - Clerk of the Board
